    *   The study could be improved by clarifying what the exact prompt engineering strategy was for getting LLMs to summarize the 'mean-field'. Different ways of summarizing/using different prompts could lead to significantly different results.

*   **Potential Influence:** MF-LLM has the potential to become a foundational framework for social simulation. The work will likely inspire further research on integrating LLMs with traditional modeling techniques, developing more effective fine-tuning algorithms for social simulations, and exploring new applications in policy evaluation and social trend forecasting.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach for simulating collective decision dynamics using LLMs. The integration of mean-field game concepts is a significant strength, and the IB-Tune algorithm provides a valuable tool for improving simulation fidelity. However, there are some limitations related to computational cost, potential bias inheritance from LLMs, evaluation methodology, and a need for more granular qualitative analysis of simulations across domains. Therefore, while this is a valuable contribution and will likely influence future research, the paper's current weaknesses prevent it from achieving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.MA
- **Score**: 7/10

### Leveraging Pre-trained Large Language Models with Refined Prompting for Online Task and Motion Planning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21596v1)
- **Authors**: Huihui Guo, Huilong Pi, Yunchuan Qin, Zhuo Tang, Kenli Li
- **Abstract**: With the rapid advancement of artificial intelligence, there is an increasing demand for intelligent robots capable of assisting humans in daily tasks and performing complex operations. Such robots not only require task planning capabilities but must also execute tasks with stability and robustness. In this paper, we present a closed-loop task planning and acting system, LLM-PAS, which is assisted by a pre-trained Large Language Model (LLM). While LLM-PAS plans long-horizon tasks in a manner similar to traditional task and motion planners, it also emphasizes the execution phase of the task. By transferring part of the constraint-checking process from the planning phase to the execution phase, LLM-PAS enables exploration of the constraint space and delivers more accurate feedback on environmental anomalies during execution. The reasoning capabilities of the LLM allow it to handle anomalies that cannot be addressed by the robust executor. To further enhance the system's ability to assist the planner during replanning, we propose the First Look Prompting (FLP) method, which induces LLM to generate effective PDDL goals. Through comparative prompting experiments and systematic experiments, we demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of LLM-PAS in handling anomalous conditions during task execution.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Leveraging Pre-trained Large Language Models with Refined Prompting for Online Task and Motion Planning":

**Summary:**

The paper presents LLM-PAS, a closed-loop task and motion planning system that integrates a pre-trained Large Language Model (LLM) to handle anomalies during task execution. Unlike traditional TAMP approaches, LLM-PAS shifts some constraint-checking responsibilities to the execution phase, allowing for exploration of the constraint space. When anomalies occur that cannot be resolved by the system's robust executors (CSubBTs), the LLM is employed to reason about the situation and generate new PDDL goals for replanning. The paper introduces First Look Prompting (FLP), a method designed to focus the LLM's reasoning on anomaly-related information, improving the quality of the generated goals.  The authors validate their approach through simulations and real-world experiments, demonstrating its effectiveness in handling various anomalies and completing tasks successfully.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   *Integration of Planning and Execution:* The paper effectively integrates planning and execution by using CSubBTs, which enhances robustness and enables exploration of the constraint space.
    *   *LLM-Assisted Replanning:* The use of an LLM for anomaly handling and replanning is a significant step forward, as it can address exceptions more flexibly than traditional methods based on pre-defined rules. The LLM facilitates generalizable solutions to anomalies, rather than hard-coded rule-based recovery, which is a significant advantage.
    *   *First Look Prompting (FLP):* The FLP method demonstrates a focused approach to prompting the LLM, improving the quality of generated PDDL goals by reducing the LLM's dependence on extraneous details and focusing on the relevant environmental context.
    *   *Empirical Validation:* The paper presents comprehensive empirical results, including comparative experiments against existing LLM-based reactive planners and ablation experiments on the FLP method. The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of LLM-PAS in handling anomalies during task execution. Both simulation and real-world experiments are included, which adds to the robustness of the claims.
    *   *Addresses a Practical Problem:* The paper addresses the practical challenges of real-world task execution, where anomalies and unexpected situations are common. LLM-PAS provides a valuable approach for handling these scenarios and improving the reliability of robotic systems.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   *LLM Limitations:* The paper acknowledges the limitations of LLMs for long-horizon planning, but it still relies on the LLM for generating PDDL goals. The performance of LLM-PAS may be affected by the LLM's reasoning capabilities and the quality of the prompts. While FLP helps, the dependence on prompt engineering remains a crucial aspect. The LLM module's failures to generate effective PDDL goals in the Object Loss Anomaly scenario are a prime example of this.
    *   *Dependency on Geometric Information:* The paper mentions but doesn't deeply explore the interaction of the LLM with the lower-level geometric constraints. The integration of symbolic and geometric reasoning could be further elaborated.
    *   *Complex Implementation:* The architecture involves multiple components: PDDLStream, CSubBT, and the LLM module. While the integration is well-described, such complexity might limit adoption.
    *   *Experiment limitations:* The simulated environment is still relatively simple compared to the complexity of real-world unstructured environments. The specific configurations tested in the real-world Action Blocking Anomaly are also constrained.
    *   *The specific actions and corresponding goal literals in Table I are missing the details for "Scan.cover."*

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The approach is novel in its focus on execution-time anomaly handling using LLMs, combined with a traditional TAMP system. The FLP method contributes to LLM-based planning by improving prompt efficiency. The integration of planning and execution, along with LLM-assisted replanning, represents a significant step in the field.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper could inspire further research on integrating LLMs with robotic systems for improved task execution and anomaly handling. The FLP method could be adopted by other researchers working on LLM-based planning. The experimental results could serve as a benchmark for future TAMP systems.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a sound approach with demonstrable improvements over existing LLM-based reactive planners. The introduction of FLP is a particularly compelling aspect, contributing to the field's understanding of effective LLM utilization. The practical experiments (simulation and real-world) demonstrate the viability of the approach. However, the inherent limitations of LLMs, dependence on prompt engineering, and the complexity of the overall system somewhat limit the paper's overall impact. There are some aspects in the paper that are not entirely correct (the specific actions in Table I). The real-world experiments, while useful, test scenarios that are relatively constrained. Despite these shortcomings, the paper advances the state-of-the-art and demonstrates a compelling vision for LLM-assisted task and motion planning.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### RDF-Based Structured Quality Assessment Representation of Multilingual LLM Evaluations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21605v1)
- **Authors**: Jonas Gwozdz, Andreas Both
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) increasingly serve as knowledge interfaces, yet systematically assessing their reliability with conflicting information remains difficult. We propose an RDF-based framework to assess multilingual LLM quality, focusing on knowledge conflicts. Our approach captures model responses across four distinct context conditions (complete, incomplete, conflicting, and no-context information) in German and English. This structured representation enables the comprehensive analysis of knowledge leakage-where models favor training data over provided context-error detection, and multilingual consistency. We demonstrate the framework through a fire safety domain experiment, revealing critical patterns in context prioritization and language-specific performance, and demonstrating that our vocabulary was sufficient to express every assessment facet encountered in the 28-question study.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an RDF-based framework for assessing the quality of multilingual Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically focusing on their handling of knowledge conflicts. The framework captures model responses under varying context conditions (complete, incomplete, conflicting, and no context) in both German and English. It uses a comprehensive RDF vocabulary adhering to FAIR principles to represent LLM evaluation results, enabling structured analysis of knowledge leakage, error detection, and multilingual consistency. The framework's effectiveness is demonstrated through an experiment in the fire safety domain using GPT-40-mini and Gemini-2.0-Flash models. The results reveal patterns in context prioritization and language-specific performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addressing a Significant Problem:** The paper tackles a crucial issue in LLM research: evaluating reliability in the face of conflicting information, especially in multilingual contexts. The focus on knowledge conflicts is particularly relevant for real-world applications where accuracy is paramount.
    *   **FAIR Principles Adherence:** The RDF-based approach aligns well with FAIR principles, promoting findability, accessibility, interoperability, and reusability of LLM evaluation data. This is a significant improvement over ad-hoc evaluation methods that often lack standardized representations.
    *   **Comprehensive RDF Vocabulary:** The proposed RDF vocabulary provides a structured way to represent various aspects of LLM evaluations, including questions, answers, validation results, and context information. This facilitates systematic analysis and comparison of different models and languages.
    *   **Multilingual Focus:** The explicit consideration of multilingual evaluations is a valuable contribution, as performance can vary significantly across languages. The language-specific extensions in the RDF vocabulary are a positive aspect.
    *   **Use Case and Experimental Validation:** The fire safety domain experiment provides a concrete demonstration of the framework's capabilities. The analysis of context prioritization and language-specific performance using SPARQL queries showcases the power of the RDF-based approach.
    *   **Clear and Well-Structured:** The paper is well-written and clearly structured, making it easy to understand the proposed framework and its evaluation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Model Set:** The evaluation only includes two LLMs (GPT-40-mini and Gemini-2.0-Flash). While the paper clearly states the goal is to demonstrate the framework, the scope of the evaluation could be broader to show the framework's utility across a more diverse set of models.
    *   **Specific Domain:** While the fire safety domain is relevant, the generalizability of the findings to other domains might be limited. Further experiments in different domains are needed to assess the framework's broader applicability.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the paper makes a solid contribution, the novelty is somewhat incremental. Other works have also explored LLM evaluation and RDF representation. The novelty lies in the combination of multilingual knowledge conflict assessment with a FAIR-aligned RDF representation tailored to LLM evaluation.
    *   **Reliance on Binary Correctness Label:** The evaluation mainly relies on binary correctness labels. In the future, it would be beneficial to extend this to fine-grained error categorization using additional metrics (e.g., semantic similarity, ROUGE) to improve the validationResult representation.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper's significance lies in its contribution to the growing field of LLM evaluation. It offers a systematic and structured approach for assessing LLM reliability, particularly in multilingual scenarios where knowledge conflicts can arise. The FAIR-aligned RDF representation promotes reproducibility and collaboration, and the fire safety domain experiment provides a valuable proof-of-concept. The framework can be used to evaluate LLM knowledge and reasoning skills, inform model development, and support the deployment of LLMs in safety-critical applications. It enhances the transparency and trustworthiness of LLM systems and provides a foundation for future research in this area.

**Rationale for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to LLM evaluation by providing a structured and FAIR-aligned framework for multilingual knowledge conflict assessment. The use of RDF and the focus on real-world challenges like knowledge conflicts are valuable aspects. While the model set is limited and the domain is specific, the paper effectively demonstrates the framework's capabilities and provides a foundation for future research. The paper addresses a critical area of LLM evaluation. The novelty lies in the integration of these elements into a cohesive, FAIR-aligned framework, rather than radical innovation on a single aspect. Overall, the paper is well-executed and contributes meaningfully to the field.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Meeseeks: An Iterative Benchmark Evaluating LLMs Multi-Turn Instruction-Following Ability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21625v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaming Wang
- **Abstract**: The ability to follow instructions accurately is fundamental for Large Language Models (LLMs) to serve as reliable agents in real-world applications. While existing instruction-following benchmarks are either single-turn or introduce new requirements in each turn without allowing self-correction, Meeseeks simulates realistic human-LLM interactions through an iterative feedback process. This design enables models to self-correct based on specific requirement failures, better reflecting real-world user-end usage patterns. The benchmark implements a comprehensive evaluation system with 38 capability tags organized across three dimensions: Intent Recognition, Granular Content Validation, and Output Structure Validation. Through rigorous evaluation across LLMs, Meeseeks provides valuable insights into LLMs' instruction-following capabilities in practical applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Meeseeks: An Iterative Benchmark Evaluating LLMs Multi-Turn Instruction-Following Ability":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Meeseeks, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate the multi-turn instruction-following abilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). Unlike existing benchmarks that primarily focus on single-turn interactions or introduce new requirements in each turn, Meeseeks simulates a more realistic human-LLM interaction through an iterative feedback process. This allows LLMs to self-correct based on specific requirement failures, mirroring real-world usage patterns. The benchmark includes a comprehensive evaluation system with 38 capability tags across three dimensions: Intent Recognition, Granular Content Validation, and Output Structure Validation. The authors evaluate a range of LLMs using Meeseeks, providing insights into their instruction-following capabilities in practical applications. Furthermore, the authors address the increased cost in the multi-turn evaluation by optimizing previous rule-augmented LLM-based evaluation to reduce costs and raise accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel approach to evaluating LLMs. Existing benchmarks largely overlook the iterative nature of human-LLM interaction, focusing on single-turn performance. Meeseeks directly addresses this gap by creating a framework where models can receive feedback and correct themselves, which is far more representative of real-world scenarios. The hierarchical capability taxonomy and the multi-round evaluation framework represent a significant departure from simpler evaluation methods. The optimization of rule-augmented LLM-based evaluation to increase performance and reduce cost is also an important contribution.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of this work lies in its potential to drive the development of more reliable and user-friendly LLM-based agents. By focusing on the iterative feedback loop, Meeseeks encourages the development of models that are better at adapting to user needs and correcting their mistakes. This is crucial for applications such as customer service, content creation, and other real-world scenarios where accurate instruction following is essential. The evaluation system provides detailed, fine-grained insights into LLM strengths and weaknesses, which can be used to guide future research and development.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Realistic Simulation:** The iterative feedback loop of Meeseeks is a significant improvement over single-turn benchmarks.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The hierarchical taxonomy of capability tags provides a granular view of LLM performance across different dimensions.
    *   **Cost Optimization:**  The integration of optimized rule-augmented LLM-based evaluation addresses the increased computational cost of multi-turn evaluation.
    *   **Insightful Results:**  The evaluation of various LLMs provides valuable insights into their strengths and weaknesses, informing future development efforts.
    *   **Open-Source Initiative Discussion:** Discussion in-depth of the concerns when open-sourcing dataset and the respective countermeasures to data leakage is valuable to the community

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Dataset:** The dataset is pre-synthesized. While parameterization adds flexibility, the lack of real-world, user-generated data may limit the generalizability of the findings. A future iteration could explore incorporating data from real-world interactions.
    *   **LLM Evaluator Dependency:**  The benchmark relies on an LLM (qwen2.5-32b-Instruct) to evaluate the responses. This introduces potential bias, as the evaluator's performance could influence the overall benchmark results. The optimization through coding the LLM-extraction process mitigates the impact by reducing the computational resources required from the evaluator but not from bias.
    *   **Computational Resources:** Despite optimizations, multi-turn evaluation remains computationally intensive. Broad accessibility is subject to resources available.

*   **Potential Influence:**  Meeseeks has the potential to significantly influence the way LLMs are evaluated and developed. Its focus on iterative feedback and fine-grained capability assessment could become a standard for future benchmarks. By encouraging the development of more adaptable and reliable models, Meeseeks could contribute to the widespread adoption of LLMs in a variety of real-world applications.

*   **Score Rationale:** Given the genuinely novel approach, significant potential to influence LLM evaluation, well-designed and comprehensive evaluation system, the discussed limitations are considerable but do not overshadow the important contributions.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Sadeed: Advancing Arabic Diacritization Through Small Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21635v1)
- **Authors**: Zeina Aldallal, Sara Chrouf, Khalil Hennara, Mohamed Motaism Hamed, Muhammad Hreden, Safwan AlModhayan
- **Abstract**: Arabic text diacritization remains a persistent challenge in natural language processing due to the language's morphological richness. In this paper, we introduce Sadeed, a novel approach based on a fine-tuned decoder-only language model adapted from Kuwain 1.5B Hennara et al. [2025], a compact model originally trained on diverse Arabic corpora. Sadeed is fine-tuned on carefully curated, high-quality diacritized datasets, constructed through a rigorous data-cleaning and normalization pipeline. Despite utilizing modest computational resources, Sadeed achieves competitive results compared to proprietary large language models and outperforms traditional models trained on similar domains. Additionally, we highlight key limitations in current benchmarking practices for Arabic diacritization. To address these issues, we introduce SadeedDiac-25, a new benchmark designed to enable fairer and more comprehensive evaluation across diverse text genres and complexity levels. Together, Sadeed and SadeedDiac-25 provide a robust foundation for advancing Arabic NLP applications, including machine translation, text-to-speech, and language learning tools.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces *Sadeed*, a new approach for Arabic diacritization based on fine-tuning a decoder-only language model (adapted from Kuwain 1.5B). The authors emphasize a careful data cleaning and normalization pipeline for training data. They claim Sadeed achieves competitive results compared to larger proprietary models and outperforms traditional models. The paper also introduces *SadeedDiac-25*, a new benchmark dataset aiming to address limitations in existing benchmarks, particularly in terms of representation of different Arabic styles (Classical and Modern Standard Arabic) and the lack of diversity in text genres. The authors perform an error analysis of existing benchmarks and release a cleaned version of the Tashkeela corpus.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a real problem:** Arabic diacritization is indeed a challenging and important NLP task, with significant downstream applications.
    *   **Emphasis on data quality:** The paper's focus on data cleaning, normalization, and careful curation of datasets is a significant strength. Poor data quality is a known bottleneck in Arabic NLP, and the authors address this head-on. The release of the cleaned Tashkeela corpus is a valuable contribution.
    *   **New Benchmark Dataset:** The SadeedDiac-25 benchmark addresses a crucial need by providing a more balanced and diverse dataset compared to existing ones. The detailed curation process, including expert review, enhances its reliability.
    *   **Competitive Results from a Small Model:** The claim of achieving competitive performance with a relatively small model (based on Kuwain 1.5B) is promising, suggesting efficient resource utilization.
    *   **Error Analysis of Existing Benchmarks:** The paper's thorough error analysis of existing benchmarks such as CATT and Abbad contributes towards increasing the reliability of future Arabic diacritization research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in Model Architecture:** The core model architecture (fine-tuning a decoder-only language model) is not particularly novel. The novelty lies more in the data handling and fine-tuning strategy. The adapted nature of the Kuwain 1.5B model, though beneficial from a practical standpoint, further reduces the architectural novelty.
    *   **Lack of Detailed Performance Comparison:** While the paper claims competitive results, more detailed comparisons against specific models (beyond those shown in the provided tables) on standardized benchmarks (Fadel is good, but more variety would be better) would be more convincing. The impact of pre-processing on the base model, Kuwain, is not thoroughly investigated.
    *   **Generalizability Concerns:** Although SadeedDiac-25 aims for broader coverage, the paper admits that *Sadeed* performs worse on Modern Standard Arabic (MSA) due to limited training data in that style. This raises concerns about the model's generalizability, even with the new benchmark.
    *   **Hallucinations:** The relatively high hallucination rate of the model is a significant limitation that needs to be addressed. The performance degradation and hallucination rates on more diverse data are concerning.
    *   **Limited Evaluation on Downstream Tasks:** While the introduction mentions the importance of diacritization for downstream tasks, the paper lacks any evaluation of *Sadeed*'s impact on those tasks. Demonstrating improvements in machine translation or text-to-speech would strengthen the significance claim.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper has the potential to influence the field by highlighting the importance of data quality in Arabic diacritization and by providing a new benchmark dataset.
    *   The successful application of a relatively small model suggests a path towards more efficient and accessible Arabic NLP solutions.
    *   The error analysis of existing benchmarks can improve future evaluation practices.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper offers practical advancements and contributes to the community by addressing data quality and benchmark limitations, but the model itself is not groundbreaking. The limited performance on MSA, the hallucination issue, and the lack of downstream task evaluations constrain its overall impact. The error analysis of other benchmark datasets and the release of a cleaned and filtered dataset is a valuable contribution. Therefore, given these considerations, a score of 6 is appropriate.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Diffusion-based Adversarial Identity Manipulation for Facial Privacy Protection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21646v1)
- **Authors**: Liqin Wang, Qianyue Hu, Wei Lu, Xiangyang Luo
- **Abstract**: The success of face recognition (FR) systems has led to serious privacy concerns due to potential unauthorized surveillance and user tracking on social networks. Existing methods for enhancing privacy fail to generate natural face images that can protect facial privacy. In this paper, we propose diffusion-based adversarial identity manipulation (DiffAIM) to generate natural and highly transferable adversarial faces against malicious FR systems. To be specific, we manipulate facial identity within the low-dimensional latent space of a diffusion model. This involves iteratively injecting gradient-based adversarial identity guidance during the reverse diffusion process, progressively steering the generation toward the desired adversarial faces. The guidance is optimized for identity convergence towards a target while promoting semantic divergence from the source, facilitating effective impersonation while maintaining visual naturalness. We further incorporate structure-preserving regularization to preserve facial structure consistency during manipulation. Extensive experiments on both face verification and identification tasks demonstrate that compared with the state-of-the-art, DiffAIM achieves stronger black-box attack transferability while maintaining superior visual quality. We also demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach for commercial FR APIs, including Face++ and Aliyun.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces DiffAIM, a new approach for protecting facial privacy against unauthorized face recognition (FR) systems. DiffAIM uses a pre-trained diffusion model to generate natural-looking yet adversarial face images. The core idea involves manipulating the facial identity within the latent space of the diffusion model, injecting gradient-based adversarial guidance during the reverse diffusion process.  This guidance optimizes for both identity convergence toward a target identity (for impersonation) and semantic divergence from the source identity (to maintain naturalness). Structure-preserving regularization is incorporated to maintain facial structure consistency. The authors demonstrate DiffAIM's effectiveness against various FR models and commercial APIs, showing superior black-box attack transferability and visual quality compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:*

The paper's core novelty lies in its use of a diffusion model for adversarial face generation with a specific focus on facial privacy protection. While other works have explored diffusion models for adversarial examples, DiffAIM distinguishes itself through its carefully designed adversarial guidance objective, which balances identity convergence, semantic divergence (using U-Net features), and structural preservation. The iterative injection of adversarial guidance during the reverse diffusion process also provides a fine-grained and controllable manipulation mechanism.  The identity-sensitive timestep truncation strategy is another novel component aimed at balancing attack effectiveness and visual realism. While each component individually isn't entirely groundbreaking, the specific combination and application to facial privacy is original.

*Significance:*

The paper addresses an important and timely problem: protecting facial privacy in the face of increasingly powerful FR systems.  The strength of the paper lies in demonstrating a significant improvement in both attack transferability (black-box attack) and visual quality compared to existing methods. This is crucial for the practical applicability of facial privacy protection techniques. The fact that the method is shown to be effective against commercial FR APIs (Face++ and Aliyun) further strengthens its significance, highlighting its potential for real-world impact. A weakness is it doesn't compare directly to similar work in the field of differential privacy to obfuscate identity. Additionally, although it demonstrates robustness to common image compression techniques, further exploration into diverse scenarios like pose variations, lighting conditions and occlusion levels is required.

*Strengths:*

*   **Strong empirical results:**  The paper presents comprehensive experimental results on both face verification and identification tasks, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance against various FR models and commercial APIs.
*   **Natural-looking adversarial faces:** The qualitative results show that DiffAIM generates more natural and less visually distorted adversarial faces compared to existing methods.
*   **Detailed ablation studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of each component of DiffAIM, justifying the design choices.
*   **Well-motivated approach:** The approach is well-motivated by the limitations of existing methods and the strengths of diffusion models.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Computational cost:**  Diffusion models are computationally expensive. The paper does not address the computational cost of DiffAIM, which could be a barrier to its practical deployment.  A discussion of the inference time and resource requirements would be valuable.
*   **Limited exploration of diverse scenarios:** While some robustness testing is included, a deeper evaluation of the method's performance under varying pose, lighting, and occlusion conditions would be beneficial.
*   **Parameter sensitivity:** The paper mentions several hyperparameters (e.g., timestep truncation ts, structure preservation regularization λ, Na, η). More discussion on the sensitivity of the results to these parameters and guidelines for setting them would improve the paper's usability.
*   **Lack of Theoretical underpinnings:** The loss functions and manipulation strategies are primarily empirically driven. The paper would benefit from a deeper theoretical analysis justifying the choices made or relating them to known adversarial attack properties.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of facial privacy protection. Its use of diffusion models offers a promising new direction for generating high-quality, transferable adversarial examples. The specific techniques developed in DiffAIM, particularly the adversarial guidance objective and structure-preserving regularization, could be adopted and extended in future research. As concerns about facial privacy continue to grow, this line of research is likely to become increasingly important.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

DiffAIM presents a novel and technically sound approach to facial privacy protection, demonstrating strong empirical results and generating visually compelling adversarial faces. The use of a diffusion model combined with the designed adversarial guidance mechanisms makes a significant contribution. The demonstrated effectiveness against commercial APIs is an important factor that sets it apart. The score is not higher because of the lack of a detailed discussion on the computational cost, theoretical analysis, and limited exploration of diverse scenarios and real-world implementation considerations. Further work will need to address these limitations to make the approach more practically viable and robust.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### HoloTime: Taming Video Diffusion Models for Panoramic 4D Scene Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21650v1)
- **Authors**: Haiyang Zhou, Wangbo Yu, Jiawen Guan, Xinhua Cheng, Yonghong Tian, Li Yuan
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of diffusion models holds the promise of revolutionizing the application of VR and AR technologies, which typically require scene-level 4D assets for user experience. Nonetheless, existing diffusion models predominantly concentrate on modeling static 3D scenes or object-level dynamics, constraining their capacity to provide truly immersive experiences. To address this issue, we propose HoloTime, a framework that integrates video diffusion models to generate panoramic videos from a single prompt or reference image, along with a 360-degree 4D scene reconstruction method that seamlessly transforms the generated panoramic video into 4D assets, enabling a fully immersive 4D experience for users. Specifically, to tame video diffusion models for generating high-fidelity panoramic videos, we introduce the 360World dataset, the first comprehensive collection of panoramic videos suitable for downstream 4D scene reconstruction tasks. With this curated dataset, we propose Panoramic Animator, a two-stage image-to-video diffusion model that can convert panoramic images into high-quality panoramic videos. Following this, we present Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction, which leverages a space-time depth estimation method to transform the generated panoramic videos into 4D point clouds, enabling the optimization of a holistic 4D Gaussian Splatting representation to reconstruct spatially and temporally consistent 4D scenes. To validate the efficacy of our method, we conducted a comparative analysis with existing approaches, revealing its superiority in both panoramic video generation and 4D scene reconstruction. This demonstrates our method's capability to create more engaging and realistic immersive environments, thereby enhancing user experiences in VR and AR applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HoloTime: Taming Video Diffusion Models for Panoramic 4D Scene Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HoloTime, a novel framework for generating immersive 360-degree 4D scenes from either user-provided or model-generated panoramic images. It leverages video diffusion models to create realistic panoramic videos, which are then transformed into 4D point clouds for scene reconstruction and rendering. The key components of HoloTime include:
1.  **Panoramic Animator:** A two-stage motion-guided image-to-video diffusion model tailored for generating high-fidelity panoramic videos.
2.  **Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction:** A method for transforming panoramic videos into 4D point clouds by leveraging space-time depth estimation.
3.  **360World Dataset:** A new large-scale dataset of fixed-camera panoramic videos with textual descriptions.
The paper claims to enable fully immersive 4D experiences, enhancing VR/AR applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**
The paper presents some novelty by integrating existing techniques to create a 4D panoramic scene.

*   **Positive Aspects:**
    *   Combining existing techniques to address the challenge of generating 4D panoramic scenes is a good attempt.
    *   The creation of the 360World dataset is a valuable contribution, addressing a specific data scarcity problem.
    *   The two-stage motion-guided generation within the Panoramic Animator seems like a reasonable approach.

*   **Negative Aspects:**
    *   The novelty lies primarily in the integration of existing techniques. The individual components (diffusion models, depth estimation, Gaussian splatting) are already established.
    *   There is a limited originality in the technical approach and the dataset.

**Significance:**

The paper's potential significance lies in advancing VR/AR applications by providing a method for generating more immersive experiences.

*   **Positive Aspects:**
    *   The generated results, though potentially enhanced with super-resolution according to the paper, are visually appealing.
    *   Provides a pipeline for generating 4D dynamic scenes from panoramic images which is a valuable step forward compared to generating just 3D static scenes.
    *   Addresses an important gap in the VR/AR field, by providing dynamic realistic scenes and a way to navigate through them.

*   **Negative Aspects:**
    *   The true impact on VR/AR experiences remains to be seen. The paper presents quantitative results but does not include detailed subjective user studies on how these generated scenes influence the immersive experience.
    *   The reliance on multiple components (diffusion models, depth estimation, optical flow, 4D Gaussian Splatting) can introduce dependencies and potential failure points.
    *   The dataset's utility heavily relies on the quality of the captured videos and their corresponding textual descriptions generated via automated process.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates a functional system and contributes a dataset filling a void in immersive VR/AR content. However, the technical novelty is incremental, primarily combining established techniques. The practical impact is promising, but not concretely demonstrated in the paper. This limits its significance within the field. The lack of detailed user studies and reliance on automated text descriptions within the dataset impacts the overall confidence in its utility. Therefore, the score reflects these limitations, leaning towards an initial contribution that requires further refinement and evaluation.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### AdaR1: From Long-CoT to Hybrid-CoT via Bi-Level Adaptive Reasoning Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21659v1)
- **Authors**: Haotian Luo, Haiying He, Yibo Wang, Jinluan Yang, Rui Liu, Naiqiang Tan, Xiaochun Cao, Dacheng Tao, Li Shen
- **Abstract**: Recently, long-thought reasoning models achieve strong performance on complex reasoning tasks, but often incur substantial inference overhead, making efficiency a critical concern. Our empirical analysis reveals that the benefit of using Long-CoT varies across problems: while some problems require elaborate reasoning, others show no improvement, or even degraded accuracy. This motivates adaptive reasoning strategies that tailor reasoning depth to the input. However, prior work primarily reduces redundancy within long reasoning paths, limiting exploration of more efficient strategies beyond the Long-CoT paradigm. To address this, we propose a novel two-stage framework for adaptive and efficient reasoning. First, we construct a hybrid reasoning model by merging long and short CoT models to enable diverse reasoning styles. Second, we apply bi-level preference training to guide the model to select suitable reasoning styles (group-level), and prefer concise and correct reasoning within each style group (instance-level). Experiments demonstrate that our method significantly reduces inference costs compared to other baseline approaches, while maintaining performance. Notably, on five mathematical datasets, the average length of reasoning is reduced by more than 50%, highlighting the potential of adaptive strategies to optimize reasoning efficiency in large language models. Our code is coming soon at https://github.com/StarDewXXX/AdaR1
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AdaR1: From Long-CoT to Hybrid-CoT via Bi-Level Adaptive Reasoning Optimization":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the efficiency bottleneck of using Long Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning in large language models (LLMs). It argues that Long-CoT isn't always necessary and can even degrade performance on simpler problems. To tackle this, the authors propose AdaR1, a two-stage framework for adaptive reasoning.  First, it creates a hybrid reasoning model by merging Long-CoT and Short-CoT models. Second, it employs bi-level preference training: (1) group-level preference guides the model to choose the appropriate reasoning style (long or short) based on the input, and (2) instance-level preference encourages concise and correct reasoning within the selected style. Experiments demonstrate reduced inference costs while maintaining performance on mathematical datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of techniques for adaptive reasoning.  Merging Long-CoT and Short-CoT isn't entirely new (model merging is an established area), but the specific application to adaptive *reasoning style* selection and the *bi-level preference training* methodology contribute significantly to the novelty.  The idea of tailoring reasoning depth to the problem is conceptually intuitive, but the realization of this idea via hybrid model merging and bi-level training is a distinct and valuable contribution. Unlike previous research focused solely on optimizing redundancy within a fixed Long-CoT process, AdaR1 strategically selects between different reasoning paths. However, the simple parameter merging used may be seen as a slight limitation in terms of novelty compared to more sophisticated merging techniques.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the growing need for more efficient LLMs, particularly for tasks requiring reasoning.  Long-CoT is computationally expensive, and AdaR1 offers a promising approach to mitigate this without sacrificing accuracy. The reported 50%+ reduction in reasoning length on mathematical datasets is substantial. The concept of adapting reasoning to problem complexity is a significant contribution, as it addresses the limitations of blindly applying Long-CoT to all tasks. If successful, AdaR1 could lead to more practical deployment of LLMs in resource-constrained environments. The release of the model weights could also facilitate further research in this area.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of Long-CoT inefficiency.
    *   **Well-Motivated Approach:** The empirical analysis convincingly demonstrates the need for adaptive reasoning.
    *   **Sound Methodology:** The bi-level preference training is a logically sound and well-explained technique.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The results show significant efficiency gains while maintaining performance.
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study provides valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Evaluation Datasets:** The primary focus on mathematical datasets may limit the generalizability of the results. It would be more convincing to expand into different domains.
    *   **Simpler Merging Technique:** The linear parameter merging, while effective, might not be the most sophisticated merging strategy. Experiments comparing to different merging techniques would be beneficial.
    *   **No comparison with CoT-Valve in the Ablation Study**: Although COT-Valve is considered a competitive baseline in the main results, it is not compared in the ablation study, which is a potential weakness.
    *   **Reproducibility:** Since the code is not yet available, reproducibility cannot be fully assessed, but the paper has included a link, so the reader has reason to expect it.

*   **Potential Impact:** AdaR1 has the potential to significantly impact the way reasoning is handled in LLMs. Its adaptive approach could be adopted as a standard practice, leading to more efficient and effective LLM deployments. Further research can explore different forms of merging, more sophisticated preference learning techniques, and application to different domains. The "Thinking Ratio" metric is also a useful contribution for analyzing reasoning styles.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:**

AdaR1 presents a significant advance in the area of efficient reasoning with LLMs. The framework is well-motivated, employs a sound methodology, and demonstrates promising results. The combination of hybrid model merging with bi-level training for adaptive reasoning is a novel contribution. While the reliance on mathematical datasets and simpler merging strategy are limitations, the impact of the method on efficiency and the potential for further research make this a high-value contribution. The score of 8 reflects the combination of strong results, solid methodology, and significant novelty while also acknowledging the identified limitations and opportunities for future work.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### From Precision to Perception: User-Centred Evaluation of Keyword Extraction Algorithms for Internet-Scale Contextual Advertising
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21667v1)
- **Authors**: Jingwen Cai, Sara Leckner, Johanna Björklund
- **Abstract**: Keyword extraction is a foundational task in natural language processing, underpinning countless real-world applications. A salient example is contextual advertising, where keywords help predict the topical congruence between ads and their surrounding media contexts to enhance advertising effectiveness. Recent advances in artificial intelligence, particularly large language models, have improved keyword extraction capabilities but also introduced concerns about computational cost. Moreover, although the end-user experience is of vital importance, human evaluation of keyword extraction performances remains under-explored. This study provides a comparative evaluation of three prevalent keyword extraction algorithms that vary in complexity: TF-IDF, KeyBERT, and Llama 2. To evaluate their effectiveness, a mixed-methods approach is employed, combining quantitative benchmarking with qualitative assessments from 552 participants through three survey-based experiments. Findings indicate a slight user preference for KeyBERT, which offers a favourable balance between performance and computational efficiency compared to the other two algorithms. Despite a strong overall preference for gold-standard keywords, differences between the algorithmic outputs are not statistically significant, highlighting a long-overlooked gap between traditional precision-focused metrics and user-perceived algorithm efficiency. The study highlights the importance of user-centred evaluation methodologies and proposes analytical tools to support their implementation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the under-explored area of user-centered evaluation for keyword extraction algorithms, particularly within the context of internet-scale contextual advertising. It compares three algorithms with varying complexity (TF-IDF, KeyBERT, and Llama 2) using both traditional precision-based metrics and qualitative user perception assessments collected through surveys. The study aims to identify factors to consider when conducting user-centered evaluations (RQ1), compare algorithm performance in both precision- and perception-based settings (RQ2), and discuss implications for real-world contextual advertising (RQ3). The key finding is that KeyBERT offers a favorable balance between performance and computational efficiency, aligning with user preferences. It also highlights the gap between traditional precision metrics and user-perceived efficiency, emphasizing the importance of human-in-the-loop evaluation and proposes analytical tools to support this.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses an Important Gap:** The paper tackles a crucial gap in the literature by focusing on user perception in keyword extraction evaluation. Most existing studies rely solely on automated metrics, which may not correlate well with real-world user experience.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The mixed-methods approach, combining quantitative benchmarking with qualitative survey experiments and novel analytical tools (horizontal/vertical analysis of survey responses), is a significant strength. The experimental design shows consideration for reducing participant fatigue and ensuring data quality.
    *   **Practical Relevance:**  The contextual advertising domain makes the work highly relevant. This provides a clear application context for the research and increases its practical impact.
    *   **Provides Useful Insights:** The paper offers practical insights into the key properties of high-quality keyword sets from a user perspective (comprehensiveness, representativeness, etc.). The identification of KeyBERT as a potentially more user-friendly algorithm in this context also has immediate practical value.
    *   **Novel Analytical Tools:** The introduction of horizontal and vertical analysis for evaluating survey responses demonstrates methodological innovation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Algorithmic Scope:** While the choice of TF-IDF, KeyBERT and Llama2 is understandable to represent families of algorithms, the paper primarily assesses one representative from each family. This limits the generalizability of the results. The study focuses on TF-IDF as the baseline statistical method, KeyBERT for a deep neural network, and Llama 2 for LLM.  This could have been supplemented with more algorithms from those families.
    *   **Subjectivity & Scope of User Studies:** The user studies, though well-designed, are inherently subjective.  The number of users (552) is relatively large, which mitigates this issue. The fact that the user demographic is mainly UK may introduce bias. While acknowledged, article topic influence (5 news articles), could be more deeply explored. The paper emphasizes the importance of gold-standard keywords stemming from annotations instead of the usual automated performance scores, but 25% of participants selected fewer than 10 words, perhaps indicating fatigue and affecting the standard for these keywords.
    *   **Ambiguity of Target Properties:** Despite efforts, there are indications from the findings that the target property of "distinctiveness" wasn't clearly understood or consistently interpreted by participants, which could impact the validity of those particular results.
    *   **Computational Cost Detail:** While the paper includes processing time for the algorithms, it would benefit from more details about hardware specifications and energy consumption for Llama 2. For instance, the results reveal a significant processing time difference, yet all algorithms were allegedly executed on the same local CPU device. Such differences may have been less significant (perhaps negligible) using parallel or GPU instances.

*   **Novelty and Significance:** The paper's main contribution lies in systematically integrating user perception into the evaluation of keyword extraction algorithms for a specific domain. While there have been user studies in information retrieval and topic modeling, this work explicitly connects user feedback to algorithm choice in the context of contextual advertising. It moves beyond simple accuracy metrics, introducing novel analysis methods for evaluating survey results and providing a more nuanced understanding of algorithm effectiveness.

**Justification for the Score:**

I assign a score of 7.5. The paper tackles a significant and under-explored problem with a well-designed methodology. The findings offer valuable insights for researchers and practitioners involved in contextual advertising and keyword extraction. The incorporation of user perception and the development of new analytical tools represent a clear contribution to the field. However, limitations in the scope of algorithms evaluated, the ambiguity of distinctiveness, and more detail on computational cost prevent it from achieving a higher score. Nevertheless, the paper opens up promising avenues for future research and development of more human-centric keyword extraction approaches.

**Score: 7.5**
- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Traceback of Poisoning Attacks to Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21668v1)
- **Authors**: Baolei Zhang, Haoran Xin, Minghong Fang, Zhuqing Liu, Biao Yi, Tong Li, Zheli Liu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) integrated with retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems improve accuracy by leveraging external knowledge sources. However, recent research has revealed RAG's susceptibility to poisoning attacks, where the attacker injects poisoned texts into the knowledge database, leading to attacker-desired responses. Existing defenses, which predominantly focus on inference-time mitigation, have proven insufficient against sophisticated attacks. In this paper, we introduce RAGForensics, the first traceback system for RAG, designed to identify poisoned texts within the knowledge database that are responsible for the attacks. RAGForensics operates iteratively, first retrieving a subset of texts from the database and then utilizing a specially crafted prompt to guide an LLM in detecting potential poisoning texts. Empirical evaluations across multiple datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of RAGForensics against state-of-the-art poisoning attacks. This work pioneers the traceback of poisoned texts in RAG systems, providing a practical and promising defense mechanism to enhance their security.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RAGForensics, a novel traceback system designed to identify poisoned texts within retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems. RAG systems are vulnerable to poisoning attacks where attackers inject malicious content into the knowledge database to manipulate the LLM's responses. Existing defenses primarily focus on inference-time mitigation, which is insufficient against sophisticated attacks. RAGForensics addresses this by iteratively retrieving subsets of texts from the knowledge database and using a specially crafted prompt to guide an LLM in detecting poisoned texts.  The paper evaluates RAGForensics against state-of-the-art poisoning attacks across multiple datasets, demonstrating its effectiveness. The work also explores adaptive attacks against RAGForensics to test its robustness. Additionally, the paper proposes a benign text enhancement strategy to improve RAG system output when faced with non-poisoned feedback.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's primary novelty lies in tackling the traceback problem for poisoning attacks in RAG systems. While poisoning attacks in machine learning and defenses against them have been studied, applying forensics techniques to RAG and developing a system to pinpoint the poisoned texts within a potentially massive knowledge database is a unique and valuable contribution.  The adaptive attacks further enhance the novelty by considering the attacker's knowledge of the traceback system.

*   **Significance:** The significance is strong because it addresses a critical security gap in RAG systems. Existing defenses mostly focus on mitigating the impact of poisoned texts *during inference*, but RAGForensics aims to *eliminate* the root cause by identifying and removing them.  This proactive approach is more effective and offers long-term security compared to merely reducing the influence of malicious content. The benign text enhancement is a useful addition, acknowledging that not all errors come from malicious data and providing a way to improve the overall system.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem and the challenges associated with tracing poisoned texts in RAG.
    *   **Technical Approach:**  RAGForensics is well-designed, combining efficient retrieval with a precise identification mechanism using LLMs. The structured prompt and iterative process are critical for minimizing false positives.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The extensive experimental evaluation across multiple datasets and poisoning attacks provides strong evidence of RAGForensics's effectiveness and robustness.
    *   **Adaptive Attacks:** The design and evaluation of adaptive attacks highlights the robustness of the proposed framework
    *   **Complete solution:** The benign text enhancement shows a strong grasp on real-world scenarios and rounds out the solution

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope (Targeted Attacks):**  RAGForensics is currently limited to targeted poisoning attacks. While it works well for scenarios where attackers aim to manipulate responses for specific queries, it's less effective against untargeted attacks where the goal is to broadly disrupt the system. The assumption of the worst-case scenario for poisoned texts (random distribution) could be restrictive. Attackers could devise more targeted distribution strategies to circumvent the proposed solution.
    *   **LLM Dependency:** The reliance on an LLM for classification introduces some inherent uncertainty. While the structured prompt and CoT help, the performance is still limited by the capabilities and biases of the LLM being used.
    *   **Performance Considerations:**  The iterative retrieval process could become computationally expensive, especially with large knowledge databases and complex queries. The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the computational costs.
    *   **Deployment challenges:** Real-world deployment may involve considerable effort to implement and requires access to user feedback, which may have privacy implications.

*   **Impact:**  This paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of secure RAG systems.  It provides a practical and promising defense mechanism that can enhance the security of RAG systems against evolving threats. The work opens up new research directions for developing more robust and reliable RAG systems. Future research will probably address the limitations outlined above, such as supporting untargeted attacks and optimizing the identification process.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign a score of **8**.  The paper offers a novel and significant contribution by introducing the first traceback system for poisoning attacks in RAG systems.  The technical approach is sound, and the empirical evaluation is comprehensive. The work addresses a critical security gap and has the potential to significantly improve the robustness and reliability of RAG systems. The adaptive attacks address a core component of system's robustness. However, the reliance on a LLM introduces a degree of uncertainty and potential biases, the limitation to targeted attacks restricts its applicability to only certain scenarios, and some considerations regarding the real-world deployment are not entirely explored.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Hoist with His Own Petard: Inducing Guardrails to Facilitate Denial-of-Service Attacks on Retrieval-Augmented Generation of LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21680v1)
- **Authors**: Pan Suo, Yu-Ming Shang, San-Chuan Guo, Xi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with external knowledge bases, improving output quality while introducing new security risks. Existing studies on RAG vulnerabilities typically focus on exploiting the retrieval mechanism to inject erroneous knowledge or malicious texts, inducing incorrect outputs. However, these approaches overlook critical weaknesses within LLMs, leaving important attack vectors unexplored and limiting the scope and efficiency of attacks. In this paper, we uncover a novel vulnerability: the safety guardrails of LLMs, while designed for protection, can also be exploited as an attack vector by adversaries. Building on this vulnerability, we propose MutedRAG, a novel denial-of-service attack that reversely leverages the guardrails of LLMs to undermine the availability of RAG systems. By injecting minimalistic jailbreak texts, such as "\textit{How to build a bomb}", into the knowledge base, MutedRAG intentionally triggers the LLM's safety guardrails, causing the system to reject legitimate queries. Besides, due to the high sensitivity of guardrails, a single jailbreak sample can affect multiple queries, effectively amplifying the efficiency of attacks while reducing their costs. Experimental results on three datasets demonstrate that MutedRAG achieves an attack success rate exceeding 60% in many scenarios, requiring only less than one malicious text to each target query on average. In addition, we evaluate potential defense strategies against MutedRAG, finding that some of current mechanisms are insufficient to mitigate this threat, underscoring the urgent need for more robust solutions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel denial-of-service attack called MutedRAG against Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. It exploits the safety guardrails of Large Language Models (LLMs) by injecting simple jailbreak prompts (e.g., "How to build a bomb") into the RAG's knowledge base. This triggers the LLM's security mechanisms, causing it to refuse to answer legitimate queries, effectively denying service. The authors show that a single malicious text can affect multiple queries due to the sensitivity of the guardrails, making the attack efficient. They evaluate MutedRAG on multiple datasets and LLMs, demonstrating high success rates and requiring fewer malicious texts compared to traditional poisoning attacks. The paper also explores potential defenses, finding current mechanisms insufficient to mitigate the threat.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of leveraging LLM safety guardrails to induce denial-of-service in RAG systems is indeed novel. Prior work primarily focused on injecting malicious content or manipulating the retrieval mechanism to influence output content. This paper turns the defensive mechanisms of LLMs into an attack vector, highlighting a previously unexplored vulnerability.

*   **Significance:** The paper's findings are significant for several reasons:

    *   **New Attack Surface:** It exposes a new and potentially more subtle attack surface in RAG systems that is directly tied to the LLM itself, rather than solely on the retrieval mechanism or knowledge base.

    *   **Efficiency:** MutedRAG's efficiency (high success rate with fewer malicious texts) makes it a practical threat. The amplification effect, where one jailbreak prompt affects multiple queries, is a concerning aspect.

    *   **Defense Challenges:** The paper's observation that existing defense mechanisms are often insufficient emphasizes the need for more robust solutions tailored to address this specific vulnerability.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and Well-Defined Attack:** The MutedRAG attack is clearly explained and easy to understand.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The extensive experimental evaluation across multiple datasets, LLMs, and attack settings (black-box, white-box) provides strong evidence for the effectiveness of the attack.
    *   **Analysis of Defenses:** The evaluation of potential defenses is a valuable contribution, highlighting the limitations of current methods and guiding future research.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The paper underscores a real-world security concern for RAG systems that rely on external knowledge.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Simplicity of Jailbreak Prompts:**  The paper primarily uses simple jailbreak prompts. While effective, further research could explore more sophisticated or adaptive jailbreak strategies and their impact on attack success.
    *   **Limited Defense Strategies:**  The defenses explored are somewhat limited. A deeper investigation into more advanced defense mechanisms (e.g., fine-tuning to improve robustness, more sophisticated filtering) would further enhance the paper.
    *   **Scope of LLMs:** While the paper examines 8 LLMs, the landscape of LLMs continues to evolve rapidly. It may be worth to examine other LLMs or even a larger variety of LLMs to make the findings even more generalizable.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field of RAG security. It will encourage researchers and practitioners to consider the security implications of LLM safety mechanisms in the context of RAG systems. It also opens up new avenues for developing more robust defense strategies.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a novel and significant contribution to the field of RAG security by identifying and exploiting a previously unexplored attack surface. The attack is efficient and poses a practical threat, and the paper provides a thorough evaluation and analysis. While there are some limitations in terms of the defense strategies explored, the overall impact and potential influence of the paper warrant a high score.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Visual Text Processing: A Comprehensive Review and Unified Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21682v1)
- **Authors**: Yan Shu, Weichao Zeng, Fangmin Zhao, Zeyu Chen, Zhenhang Li, Xiaomeng Yang, Yu Zhou, Paolo Rota, Xiang Bai, Lianwen Jin, Xu-Cheng Yin, Nicu Sebe
- **Abstract**: Visual text is a crucial component in both document and scene images, conveying rich semantic information and attracting significant attention in the computer vision community. Beyond traditional tasks such as text detection and recognition, visual text processing has witnessed rapid advancements driven by the emergence of foundation models, including text image reconstruction and text image manipulation. Despite significant progress, challenges remain due to the unique properties that differentiate text from general objects. Effectively capturing and leveraging these distinct textual characteristics is essential for developing robust visual text processing models. In this survey, we present a comprehensive, multi-perspective analysis of recent advancements in visual text processing, focusing on two key questions: (1) What textual features are most suitable for different visual text processing tasks? (2) How can these distinctive text features be effectively incorporated into processing frameworks? Furthermore, we introduce VTPBench, a new benchmark that encompasses a broad range of visual text processing datasets. Leveraging the advanced visual quality assessment capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs), we propose VTPScore, a novel evaluation metric designed to ensure fair and reliable evaluation. Our empirical study with more than 20 specific models reveals substantial room for improvement in the current techniques. Our aim is to establish this work as a fundamental resource that fosters future exploration and innovation in the dynamic field of visual text processing. The relevant repository is available at https://github.com/shuyansy/Visual-Text-Processing-survey.
- **Summary**: This paper presents a comprehensive survey of visual text processing, covering text image reconstruction and manipulation tasks. It categorizes text features (structure, stroke, semantics, style) and learning paradigms (architecture, loss function, learning strategy, data representation) used in different methods. The paper introduces VTPBench, a new benchmark for evaluating visual text processing models across various tasks, and proposes VTPScore, an evaluation metric leveraging multimodal large language models (MLLMs).  The authors then evaluate over 20 models using VTPBench and VTPScore, identifying areas for improvement. The paper aims to serve as a resource for future research in visual text processing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in two aspects: (1) providing a unified survey covering both text image reconstruction and manipulation, unlike previous surveys that focused mainly on text spotting; and (2) introducing VTPBench and VTPScore. The taxonomy of text features and learning paradigms, while helpful, is not entirely novel as similar categorizations exist in other surveys on related topics. The use of MLLMs for evaluation is a growing trend, but VTPScore represents a specific application and refinement of this idea within the context of visual text processing.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from addressing the fragmentation in visual text processing research and evaluation. By providing a unified benchmark and a standardized evaluation method, it aims to improve the fairness and reliability of performance comparisons. This could lead to more focused research efforts and the development of more robust models. However, the true impact will depend on the adoption of VTPBench and VTPScore by the research community.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive coverage of the literature.
    *   Clear categorization of text features and learning paradigms.
    *   Introduction of a unified benchmark (VTPBench) and evaluation metric (VTPScore).
    *   Empirical evaluation of various models using the proposed benchmark.
    *   Clear identification of open challenges and future research directions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The categorization of text features, while helpful, doesn't provide groundbreaking insights.
    *   The novelty of VTPScore is somewhat limited by the existing trend of using MLLMs for evaluation in other domains.
    *   The paper acknowledges that there is "room for improvement" of many of the text visual process models currently being used, without directly offering improvements.
    *   The long-term impact depends on adoption by the community and ongoing maintenance and expansion of VTPBench.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to be a highly cited reference for researchers working on visual text processing. By providing a single point of access for a wide array of techniques, it simplifies the landscape for new entrants. However, its true impact will depend on the research community's willingness to embrace VTPBench.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses outlined above, the paper represents a solid contribution to the field. While the individual components (taxonomy, using MLLMs for evaluation) are not groundbreaking, the combination of these elements into a unified survey, benchmark, and evaluation framework provides clear value. The paper offers a useful synthesis of existing knowledge and a practical resource for future research. There is, however, space for refinement and the benchmark requires the adoption of the research community to become the standard it is proposed to be.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### XBreaking: Explainable Artificial Intelligence for Jailbreaking LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21700v1)
- **Authors**: Marco Arazzi, Vignesh Kumar Kembu, Antonino Nocera, Vinod P
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models are fundamental actors in the modern IT landscape dominated by AI solutions. However, security threats associated with them might prevent their reliable adoption in critical application scenarios such as government organizations and medical institutions. For this reason, commercial LLMs typically undergo a sophisticated censoring mechanism to eliminate any harmful output they could possibly produce. In response to this, LLM Jailbreaking is a significant threat to such protections, and many previous approaches have already demonstrated its effectiveness across diverse domains. Existing jailbreak proposals mostly adopt a generate-and-test strategy to craft malicious input. To improve the comprehension of censoring mechanisms and design a targeted jailbreak attack, we propose an Explainable-AI solution that comparatively analyzes the behavior of censored and uncensored models to derive unique exploitable alignment patterns. Then, we propose XBreaking, a novel jailbreak attack that exploits these unique patterns to break the security constraints of LLMs by targeted noise injection. Our thorough experimental campaign returns important insights about the censoring mechanisms and demonstrates the effectiveness and performance of our attack.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "XBreaking: Explainable Artificial Intelligence for Jailbreaking LLMs" proposes a novel jailbreaking attack on Large Language Models (LLMs) that leverages Explainable AI (XAI) to understand and exploit the behavioral differences between censored (safe) and uncensored versions of the same model. The approach, named XBreaking, involves using XAI techniques to fingerprint the internal representations of both censored and uncensored LLMs, identifying key layers responsible for content filtering, and then injecting targeted noise into these layers to bypass security constraints. The authors conduct experiments on several open-source LLMs, demonstrating the effectiveness of their attack and providing insights into the censoring mechanisms of these models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a relatively novel approach to LLM jailbreaking by explicitly focusing on explainability-guided noise injection. While the concept of adversarial attacks on LLMs is well-established, the combination of XAI with white-box layer perturbation adds a new dimension. Existing jailbreak approaches predominantly operate under a black-box framework, trying different input prompts to determine which can bypass the censoring mechanisms. Using white-box access with XAI strategies offers an advantage that allows for a deeper understanding of the censoring mechanisms and provides a more targeted attack. This focus sets it apart from many existing jailbreaking methods.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to improve our understanding of LLM security. By reverse-engineering the censoring mechanisms using XAI, the authors provide valuable insights for both attackers and defenders. It highlights the vulnerabilities of current alignment techniques and emphasizes the need for more robust defense strategies. The demonstration of a successful jailbreak attack through layer perturbation also has practical implications for security practitioners. It shows that LLMs are susceptible to targeted attacks even with advanced censoring techniques.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper provides a well-defined methodology for the XBreaking attack, outlining the steps involved in fingerprinting, layer selection, and noise injection.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The authors conduct experiments on a variety of open-source LLMs, providing empirical evidence for the effectiveness of their attack.
    *   **Insights into Censoring Mechanisms:** The XAI analysis provides valuable insights into how LLMs filter harmful content, which could be useful for developing more robust defenses.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The experimental evaluation is limited to a few open-source LLMs. It is unclear how well the XBreaking attack would generalize to larger, more complex proprietary models. The open-source LLMs analyzed have significantly fewer parameters than propriety LLMs, such as ChatGPT and Gemini. It is unclear if the results will scale to larger language models as is.
    *   **Reliance on White-Box Access:** The XBreaking attack requires white-box access to both censored and uncensored models. In many real-world scenarios, attackers may only have access to the censored model, limiting the applicability of the approach. Also, acquiring an uncensored version of a large language model to implement this attack would be quite difficult given that only a few organizations have the resources to train these models. This is a significant limitation for this strategy in a real-world scenario.
    *   **Overestimation Concerns:** The use of LLM-as-a-Judge raises concerns about bias in the evaluation. While the authors attempt to validate the LLM judge, there is still a possibility that it overestimates or underestimates the harmfulness of the generated content. LLM-as-a-Judge is shown to be a good heuristic, but given the importance of the subject it is hard to consider this to be a rigorous approach.
    *   **Perturbation Mechanism:** The proposed perturbation mechanism is relatively simplistic. The paper uses basic Gaussian noise. More sophisticated adversarial perturbation techniques could potentially yield even better results.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could influence future research in LLM security. It could lead to the development of more robust defense mechanisms based on explainability. It could also inspire new jailbreaking techniques that leverage more sophisticated adversarial methods.
*   **Missing Literature:** There is a growing body of research in LLM security, including jailbreaking and defense techniques. It would be helpful to the reader if these papers were included and discussed within the paper.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **6** to this paper.
